The central themeofthe study is the formulation ofaSMART (sustainable, measurable, attainable, realizable, and time-bounded) Comprehensive Land Use Plan which will guide, control and regulate the City of Manila's future growth and development. The study has been therefore structured around this central theme and the methodology, and the time spent on the main activities, relate directly to the need to evaluate and resolve alternative strategies. These strategies will be complemented by shorter term proposals which would apply immediate improvements.

For three years, the country chafed under the Japanese occupation. The end of the Pacific War left Manila in ruins but it also brought liberation and independence. In July of 1946, the Commonwealth government under Manuel L. Quezon declared independence.

HISTORICAL DEVELOPMENT

Manila began as a small tribal settlement on the banks of the Pasig River near the mouth of Manila Bay. It took its name from a white-flowered mangrove plant , known as the Nilad, that grew in abundance in the area. Maynilad, "where the nilad grows", wasa fairly prosperous Islamic community ruled by Rajah Sulayman, a descendantof a royal Malay family.

MANILA: COMPREHENSIVE

The post-war years saw the reconstruction of Manila and its growth in area and population. Land was developed in areas now covered by the Municipality of San Juan.

LAND, USE PLAN

###### AND YONING © ORDINANCE

Subdivisions and residential villages flourished as well in Quezon City, Pasig, Pasay

On May 24, 1570, almost 50 years after Ferdinand Magellan, a Portuguese explorer under the service of the King of Spain, first set foot on these islands, a Spanish expedition under Marshal Martin de Goiti reached Sulayman's settlement. Encountering resistance from the Muslim king, de Goiti retaliated by burning down villages and capturing the artillery. The following year, Spanish conquistador Miguel Lopez de Legazpi arrived at the mouth of the Pasig River and claimed the islands in the name of the King of Spain. He established the “distinguished and ever loyal city" of Manila, proclaiming it as the capital. Thus began the Spanish colonization of the Philippines of almost 333 years.

and Parafaque. Factories and industrial areas burgeoned in Caloocan, Malabon and Valenzuela. Adjoining municipalities of Las Pifias, Muntinlupa, Taguig, Pateros and Marikina were developed and annexed. In 1976, a conglomerationoffour cities - Manila, Pasay, Caloocan and Quezon City - and 13 municipalities was officially designated as “Metro Manila".

###### Buhayin ang Maynila! @

Today, Metro Manila is also known as the National Capital Region, a thriving, ever enlarging urban sprawl covering about 630 square kilometers and harboring a population of approximatelyten million.

The new city was encircled by double walls, the Intramuros, and guarded by a fort, the Fort

Santiago. The Spanish kept to their enclave and sent out their missionaries and armies to

conquer the countryside. In the suburbs or arabales like Tondo, Santa Cruz, Quiapo,

Sampaloc and Malate, the indios, as the natives were called, lived and worked together with the mestizos (of mixed Filipino and foreign descent). The sangleys or Chinese merchants

lived in the parian, a district which became part of the present Binondo.

In the 19th century, Spain's colonies were racked by corrupt administration and internal disorder. Liberal ideologies fired the spirits of enlightened Manilefios

like Philippine national hero Jose Rizal who studied abroad and Filipino rebel leader Andres Bonifacio who read books on revolutionaries and philosophers. The seeds of revolution were thus sown in Philippine soil, and insurrection sprouted all over the countryside. By the late 1800s, Spain had lost control over the Philippines, and with her major defeat by the American fleet in the Battle of Manila Bay, totally relinquished her hold on the colony.

History of the City of Manila

But freedom would not come so easily, for the Filipinos eventually found themselves under their erstwhile ally, the Americans. Under the new conquerors, Manila spread outwards, roads and bridges were built, and schools taught the Filipinos Western culture and proficiency in a new language, English. Democratic processes were introduced; and neoclassical government edifices rose around the old city. The outbreak of World War II soon halted all that.

MANILA TODAY

The City of Manila today has its strong points. It is a cultural center teeming with culture and tradition. It is a center for popular culture as well as an educational center. It plays host to 41 universities and colleges in Metro Manila with students coming from all over the Philippines. It

The Transition Zone includes the Sta. Ana and Pandacan districts of Manila from Nagtahan Bridge to Lambingan Bridge. Land uses are principally industrial and low income

residential areas. There is much vacant land, derelict and underused industrial sites, squatter and low income housing. This area also has the most oil depots and other related

Development has concentrated along the waterfront area and along Pasig River with an agglomeration of mixed activities and land uses at the areas of Binondo, Quiapo and Sta. Cruz. In infrastructure, two (2) major transportation corridors are expected to trigger commercial development. These are the Ramon Magsaysay Boulevard where the ongoing LRT 2 will link the City to the northeastern region specifically to Antipolo and greater Rizal province. The other is the proposed LRT4 that will run from Fairview channeling through Quezon Boulevard and to Espana Boulevard. Both Light Railways will crisscross at the site of the Old Bilibid Prison property.

Along the Pasig River, Urban Renewal Projects were already identified as previously discussed in the Pasig River Rehabilitation Master Plan. It encompasses different zones of development and has identified a number of historically significant structures.

The Heritage Zone encompasses the principal historic/heritage sites and adjoining urban built-up areas within Central Manila from the mouth of Manila Bayto250 m beyond Nagtahan Bridge. This includes the Port Area and the historical areas of Intramuros, San Nicolas, Quiapo and San Miguel, areas which have suffered a consistent decline as business and visitor centers. Problems in this zone include poor building fabric, riverbank squatting, polluted esteros and inadequate infrastructure. Emphasis with this zone is primarily the revitalization of small and mid-scale commercial uses. Secondary uses are tourism, recreation, institutional, low-residential and low intensity manufacturing. Opportunities for historic preservation, adaptive reuse and open space abound.

In a Nutshell: In a Theoretical Square, the present development structure of the city is a complete segregation of place of work and place of residence. The residential development is concentrated on the outer sphere or peripheral boundary while the places of work and commercial activities are concentrated at the inner core. This spatial structure creates an agglomeration of services at the inner core only that in effect causes traffic problems going inwards at daytime and a struggle outward at night time.

Agglomeration of Services

Major causes of the annual flooding of low-lying areas in Manila must also be addressed. These are caused by two factors: 1) overflowing of the Pasig River and esteros during intense storms, and 2) inadequate inland drainage facilities to cope with excessive local surface run-offs. The areas that are frequently and oftentimes seriously flooded are Tondo, Sta. Cruz, Sampaloc, Sta. Mesa and SanAndres.

OPPORTUNITIES

STRENGTHS-OPPORTUNITIES STRATEGIES

Designation of an area for construction of a CBD (identification of an optimal/feasible/most viable place)

Install adequate provision of services to support the upcoming

CBD and prepare the area for the influx of people (preparation for future developments)

Repair the district's identity to encourage the people to live,

work, and use the area (increase usability) Ensure proper maintenance and responsibility for the river/estero of those who use or live near it Provision of pedestrian network as a medium of exploration Constant maintenance of drainage system Provision of more health services and medical personnel Proper landscaping of the sidewalks

WEAKNESSES-OPPORTUNITIES STRATEGIES

Clear/repair and maintain regularly all drainage systems as to fully utilize its capacity/effectivity

Inform and educate all citizens (formal and informal) about proper solid waste disposal and management Impose traffic regulatory measures like coding, one-way schemes, time management, first-come-first-serve, etc. Encourage pedestrianization (make the city more pedestrian friendly by installing facilities) as to reduce the volume of vehiclesinthearea Continuous proper maintenance of the traffic facilities (e.g. lights) to increase effectivity and install them on areas that need them (traffic congestion) Identify areas to be possibly used to increase the efficiency in traffic flow and develop them ( e.g. road widening, pavement strengthening) Instill among the citizens/locals community participation/involvement and responsibility for their own area Employ programs for adequate provision of goods and services for the whole community ( e.g. health, markets, education) Ensure all activities are environment-friendly in terms of their emission, resource use and disposal (proper accreditation, auditing for the certification of the ISO) Impose regular maintenance and monitoring of all facilities as to prolong their usage and effectivity and decrease the potential

costsforrepairs

- * — Active community participation
- * Development of academic center (“Silicon Valley”/ IT District)
- * — Rezoning according to district neighborhoods ¢ University Belt area

THREATS

- * Low drainage capacity
- * — Informal settlers account to 18, 304 (3.74 % of Manila’s total, least)
- * — High crime rate (petty larson, snatching)

development instead of carrying waste and sewage. At present, Pasig River is categorized as class C water or only for navigation use. The riverfront should be enhanced with well-lit outdoor cafes or commercial activitiesthat willenhance its image. Develop this side of Manila as a transportation corridor or at least become a city by the river to push away industrial activities at the outskirts of the city and encourage linear parks and major activity places.

- A. Emerging Issues and Concerns

1. Urbanization has led to the dense built-up nature of the city and deterioration has

already started.

Problems like flooding, deterioration of external environment (buildings), inefficient

utilities support, degradation of the natural environment, and loss of the City's

imageability* and usability in terms of its unique characteristic.

- B. Goals Regain the City's imageability (visual impression) Employ the maximum potential of the city's accessibility Regain the city's lost usability

- 1.
- 2.
- 3.

- C. Objectives ite

DorPo

To encourage mixed use development for residential, commercial and institutional land

uses that would create less disturbances and a better connectivity to the existing

activities and accommodate future growth in an orderly fashion.

To rationalize the existing road network in order to improve mobility and to distribute

development systematically.

To identify and develop open spaces within the city into urban parks and parking spaces.

To equip the city against the existing and potential development constraints.

To create distinction per district and exhibit their unique characteristics.

To improve the aesthetic features of the city.

- D. Strategies 1.

and air pollution. Compact development pattern supports more cost-effective infrastructure than low-density fringe development and will bolster full use of urban services.

###### Buhayin angMaynila! &

Consider careful street sizing and accommodation of some parking on streets.

Streets should be sized for their use: lower density areas that have little through traffic are best served by slower, narrower streets, while transportation corridors that move district wide traffic need wider travel ways.

Coordination of land use and transportation to manage growth.

Mixed Uses: houses and shopping - Because of its scale, the urban village will

have a mixture of uses within close walking distance of each other---or even down

the flightof stairs.

10.

Mixed Uses: shops and transport- another place to mix uses is at transportation hubs. What could be more logical than putting daily shopping where you change mode of transport?..it is an obvious convenience and it makes taking public transit more feasible because it saves time.

Encourage mixed-use development through Transport Oriented Development (TOD) along major thoroughfares and LRT lines. (Espafia Boulevard and Ramon Magsaysay Boulevard).

###### Strategic Planning Physical Characteristics

Areas in transition along emerging growth centers and mass transit stations call for densification of land usage. In the 1 km radial distance from a mass transit station, high density land uses should be accommodated.

. Create Urban spaces in each district to form an important component of the urban fabric. Their function varies from the main public square expressive of civic dignity and status, to the humblest quiet corner. Spaces may be for relaxation,

alternative to the corridor street and a greater variety of spatial experience.

- 19. Turning around inner-city neighborhoods into new directions. To improve the Manila residents’ living conditions and their access to a wider range of social and economic opportunities would need the following:

e Economic and social diversity. The concentration of poor families in isolated

enclaves is destructive from a societal as well as an individual standpoint. To attract a more diverse population and make the communities more supportive and attractive places in which to live, certain kinds of development should be encouraged a mix of land uses; diverse housing types at a range of prices; neighborhood-serving stores and services; and a full range of community

facilities, services, and "social infrastructure." Furthermore,the government at

all levels should review policies and programs that encourage concentration of

the poor.

Physical Improvements. Specific physical improvements are needed to

improve both the appearances and function of the community. Among the typical physical needs of these neighborhoods were removal of blight, rehabilitation of salvageable deteriorated properties, infill residential and commercial development, street improvements and sometimes street rerouting; and beautification of the community through provision of greenspace, landscaping, streetscape improvements, and public amenities such as plazas,

lakes and entertainment areas.

Certain design improvements were also commonly needed, for example, the

clustering of commercial development in nodes at intersections along

underused commercial corridors; the creation of "town centers" incorporating a

variety of land uses, activities, and services; and more mixed-use

developments where appropriate.

e Improved economic opportunities.

e Public and social services.

- 20. For Public Space -People naturally meet and talk where they park their cars. Therefore, design parking lots as common areas that are symbolically and physically accessible urban outdoor rooms, that are delightful places to talk, have a full palette of sensory experiences, and include a comfortable microclimate. Provide for the comfort and dignity of the citizen as pedestrian and as a driver.

Informal Spaces - Informal spaces are as necessary to the urban scene as formal spaces. Although more relaxed in feeling, they have often been deliberately

MANILA COMPREHENSIVE LAND, USE” PLAN

planned in response to specific needs, and have developed their own individual character for that reason.

- 26. Cornerstone Parking: Design temporary parking lots as the framework for later construction. Establish patterns of circulation and a tradition of use through events such as food vendors, farmers' markets, orfestivals.
- 27. Street yards: Residential streets should be neighborhood commons in which pedestrians have the right-of-way and the driver is a guest. Parking can be located to help slow down traffic, to provide alcoves off the circulation route for play, and to replace offstreet lots.
- 28. Shop-House: Houses that sit above streetfront garages should be designed and zoned to allow home offices and shops in and adjacent to the garage. The garage should have ample light, ventilation, and other features ofapleasant room. There should also be separate access points to the different spaces/uses in the shophouse.
- 29. Zoning the Sidewalk: Pedestrianization is encouraged and so the provision of a safe environment and frailties for the pedestrian is important. Therefore, zoning the sidewalk into a main walking aisle, areas for activities, street furniture, utility access, and so forth is to be developed or implemented to ensure the provision of comfortto the pedestrians.

- 21. Recognize that within the downtown area of Manila, the majority of trips are on foot and that a significant number of vehicular trips are by bicycle and local public transit. Build generous and delightful walkways (arcaded walks) with a minimum of curb cuts for vehicles. Provide bicycle parking, bike lanes, and bike-activated traffic signals if possible. Give mass transit priority over car traffic with bus malls. Provide curb parking and public garages or alley lots to support pedestrian

access.

- 22. Ensure that a walkable district will be provided for the entire city especially in areas in the coverage radius of a transport terminal. Provide neighborhood facilities at the core and pastoral amenities at the periphery. Walkways, bicycle parking, and automobile drop-offs should have direct access to the station. Ifa large park-and-ride lot is necessary, it should double as fairgrounds, farmers' market or vendors' alley, and/or other public gathering place and include an orchard or other garden.
- 23. Modifying the Strip: Work to (re) introduce the pedestrian into existing commercial auto strips. Provide pleasant walks and bike paths from adjacent neighborhoods to encourage those nearby to walk. Alter zoning regulations to eliminate minimum parking requirements, place parking size on rear of buildings, allow mixed-use and multistory development. Look for existing nodes of pedestrian-friendly buildings and encourage their growth.
- 24. Campus: Build car-free gardens and grounds that housea set of buildings for an institution. Construct a well-defined edge to the campus but do not isolate it from the city or district with a ring of parking lots. Instead, edge the campus with streetcar streets, local main street, or neighborhoods with live-end streets. Provide hidden parking and courtyard parking in pockets outside of and within the campus. Provide many parking and transportation options and coordinate services with adjoining neighborhood.
- 25. Industrial Spines: Compose industrial districts along main transportation routes such as highways, railways, and ports. Provide factory outlets, restaurants, and other support services along intersecting local roads. Concentrate truck parking in work yards along industrial road, and place well-forested employee parking as

- 1. In view of its young population, the City of Manila should strengthen its education, health and welfare systems. The huge demand for education and health services will have to be fulfilled in order to develop the human resource base.
- 2. Provide more public utilities and for its improvement in areas of dense population and human activity such as commercial districts and university districts.
- 3. In conjunction with providing or improving public utilities in dense areas, the local government should rationalize population distribution and density among the districts. This may entail providing incentives to residents (such as better housing in alternative areas) for them to relocate in the less dense areas or defining certain zones as medium density residential areas.
- 4. Redevelop the city's inner core. Historically, the inner core of older cities tends to be a place of neglect and out-migration. The city government can redevelop the urban core to encourage residents to return or relocate to this area but should set population density limits through appropriately zoned areas.

While the Manila Health Department has an adequate number of physicians and nurses, based on HLURB health sector standards, more midwives are needed, as the standard HLURB ratio is one midwife per 5,000 population. Midwives are essential in family planning and maternal and child health programs. Midwives and other trained birth attendants are also crucial during home births, given the likelihood of sepsis and tetanus in unsanitary birthing procedures and conditions.

Plans and Programs

2005-2020 57

- 1. Walking District. Conduct a study on specific areas of the City that incur high human activity wherein in order to control vehicular traffic vehicular access limits can be imposed. In line with the concept of TODs, here parking areas are provided for in the outer limits of the district and walking is encouraged to go through the area or use of the mass transport facilities. Parking facilities at development nodes should also be developed with a management system to regulate on-street parking. Adetailed study should be undertaken over the next two years to define and determine the optimal location for the parking facility locations.
- 2. Roads and Bridges Rehabilitation Drive. A program where the community and government work hand-in-hand in maintaining the good condition of transport infrastructures.
- 3. Traffic Improvement and Management Project .The entire network of roads as well as the proposed public transport terminal studies will be taken into consideration in developing a matrix of traffic intervention. The projects aims to improve the flow of vehicular traffic at congested intersections through short-term infrastructure projects suchas signalization, channelization and grade-separation.
- 4. Upgrading of Water Transport Docking Facilities Project. The project is aimed at improving the accessibility of the CBD via the River system. Being a component of a bigger renewal program, the project will entail coordination with national agencies. The project involves the construction of covered platforms along the Pasig River.
- 5. Structure Access Plan. It was difficult to analyze the traffic volume following the classical traffic modeling exercises, given the available data and information on Manila . In many previous planning exercises, growth in passenger traffic was forecasted as a derived function of population growth, growth in income and income elasticity of demand for transport. Since very little data is available on per capita income and considering the anticipated changes in urban activities and the declining historical population trend, any forecasted values would be subject to a significant amountoferror.This is further compounded by the fact that the

x a |

i

- D. Strategies

- 1. Seek the cooperation of all appropriate public and private entities in the development of transit system from the Coastal Road and from major entryway of the city to the inner core to different Barangays and adjacent towns. This system should provide frequent and dependable service.
- 2. Land use policies and regulations are to be developed to ensure that new Multi-family and commercial development is located in areas that encourage pedestrian movement butin any event are serviceable by public transit.
- 3. Develop asystem of pedestrian and bike trails from motorized vehicles where possible.
- 4. Create areas that prevent motorized vehicular access and instead place car storage facilities (parking buildings) around these areas to encourage walking and decrease vehicular traffic in densely populated areas and areas of high activity.
- 5. Provide inter-modal public transportation terminals.
- 6. Consider the pursuance of the PRRMP proposed ferry terminals along the Pasig River as means of transport and upgrade the existing water transport docking facilities.
- 7. Identify backstreets that can be used as alternate routes in order to diffuse traffic in major streets.
- 8. Considerthe placement of transport facilities underground (LRT).
- 9. Provision of on-street pay parking.

FLOOD CONTROL

- 3. Proper design of sediment basins. There is a need to conceptualize catch basins that trap wastes and facilitate easy removal. The sizes of inlets to these basins should be designed properly in term of opening lengths, and spacingofinlets.
- 4. Information and Education Campaign (IEC) on Drainage. Information and Education campaigns will provide inhabitants with a greater understanding of the significance of flood control and where cooperation is needed. In view of the flooding problems of Manila, cooperation is needed in the following :

- - Collection and disposal of solid waste-( garbage, refuse, construction materials and debris)
- - Discouraging occupancy along river easements
- - Use ofa two to three vault septic tank to undergo pre-treatment of liquid waste

- 5. Public Market Solid and Liquid Waste Management System. This requires coordination with vendors and buyers in public markets, flea markets and street vending areas. It is important that those engaged in retailing ( or wholesale) realize that improper collection and disposal of wastes find their way in street sewers and ultimately in the water bodies near them.
- 6. Formulation of guidelines towards Debris Control/ Sediment Control and Proper Disposal of Wastes. There is a need for sediment control or debris control to be effected in support of the declogging, resizing, dredging projects that the city may pursue. This may be in the formof:

- A. Emerging Issues and Concerns

1. Wide coverage of flood risks

Presence of clogged drainage system and esteros

Present drainage facilities can no longer efficiently accommodate present and future

needs asa flood control infrastructure

- B. Goals

- 1. Ensure the free flow of water through the drainage system
- 2. Minimize the flooded areas

- C. Objectives

- 1. Improvement of the flood control facilities
- 2. Removal of obstructions in the drainage system
- 3 Identification and implementation of flood alleviation measures

- D. Strategies

- 5. Publication of Flood Risk Map Flood risk map is recommended to be published and disseminated to the

inhabitants and governmental agencies concerned. The flood risk map will inform Manilenos about the flood risk of their locality and will aid them in preparing for the flood with the establishment of evacuation routes and

evacuation areas. A flood risk map shall be prepared for each drainage district

(not political district) and proper organization shall be instituted. For its

preparation, the city must conduct surveys of the sewershed to properly identify

and assign communities to drainage areas. This should also aid in controlling the

relative elevations which change when new constructions are made or changes

in the physical structure of the drainage network are effected.

ENVIRONMENTAL MANAGEMENT CHARACTERISTICS

E. Plans and Programs

- 1. IEC on Environmental Stewardship.
- 2. Environmental Awareness Projects. Projects wherein the primary goal is to uphold the quality of the environment where all constituents of the city is involved.
- 3. Local Environmental Monitoring Organization.
- 4. Strict enforcement of ISO standards through ISO certification of all industries and businesses located in periphery of the water bodies. ISO certification of other industries is also encouraged.
- 5. Observance of Proper Easements on Water Bodies. This is to reduce the coincidences of polluting the waters. The most frequent polluter are the squatter settlement/informal settlement for they do not practice proper solid waste management and sanitation practices due to lack of facilities.

- A. Emerging Issues and Concerns

- 1. Urbanization has imposed dangers to the natural environment in the City.
- 2. People are forgetting that they too are part of the environment.

1. Maintain the succession of the natural environment with the progress of the

urban environment

- C. Objectives

1. To assure a successive ecological balance between the urban environment and

the natural environment.

- D. Strategies

- 1. Plant trees in the urban environment as bird sanctuary and it would also add to the aesthetic value of the area.
- 2. Construction of man-made coralline system for the assurance of marine life succession.
- 3. Removal of stagnation through regular street flushing/cleaning.
- 4. Formulation of an ordinance proclaiming areas only for the sole purpose of providing a place for the natural environment to occupy and propagate (e.g. Parks).
- 5. Formation of a local body of a multi-partite nature promoting and conserving the natural environment within the city.
- 6. Conduct an IEC regarding the relevance of the natural environment co-existing with the urban environment and its difference.

The Solid Waste Management for the City of Manila involves the important task for the disposal of the large amount of refuse effectively while meeting hygienic

From the hands of the residents, the garbage is now segregated in the hands of the Barangay Official where a larger garbage collection facility per Barangay Zone is installed at a location that would serve the covered barangays efficiently. Waste segregation

Illustrated hereafter is a Solid Waste Collection Scheme where the Citizens are involved in making Solid Waste Collection more efficient. The basic waste

###### Buhayin ang Maynila! &

management measures that are involved in this plan are the 3R's concept (Recycle, Re-use, Reduce) which leads to the main goal of Source Reduction.

At each level of garbage collection centers, recoverable materials may be sold to prospect industries/establishments in need of these materials for processing/production. And the barangay can use the money gained from this endeavorand so on.

Recycling means taking apart and old product and using the material it contains to

make a new product. To Reduce, on the other hand, implies waste reduction by not

producing in the first place. And Re-use means repeated useofan item rather than throwingitaway.

And if this practice is fully implemented, further segregation activities can be performed, e.g.As illustrated in the 15-20 year scheme.

The main component of this scheme is the participation and initiative of the

Citizens.

Certain guidelines regarding the waste collection and storage in the barangay level must be followed to avoid the spread of odors, illnesses, and pests within the community.

The plan starts at the household where residents are encouraged to segregate their waste into certain categories such as Biodegradable, Non-biodegradable and Recyclable. Biodegradable refuse are those of organic origin and those that can be degraded/decomposed by the earth in a short period of time such as food waste. Non-biodegradable refuse are items that takes very long period of time to degrade/decompose such as plastics and metals.

From segregation at home, residents are asked to bring their own garbage to the specifically assigned Barangay Hall where they have facilities for collecting a type of waste. Each barangay hall can only accept a type of waste where classification or categorization of waste is the same as that of the household segregation scheme. In place of the barangay hall where areas are not accessible to them, e.g. informal settlements, installation of communal garbage bins per type of waste can

###### Buhayin ang Maynila! &

- - IECon waste segregation (recyclable, biodegradable, nonbiodegradable)
- - promotion of “money in waste” through programs encouraging finding/using waste materials as livelihood resource (eg crafts)
- - Manufacture and sale of products wherein packaging/containers can be re-used

- - promote use of recyclable materials (especially households, commercial developments, and manufacturing industries)
- - Manufacture and sale of environment-friendly products (eg reduced packaging materials, biodegradables)
- - limit items sold w/in the city wic is not environment friendly (recyclable, of recycled materials)
- - IEC on community participation in the maintenance of their environment (environmental stewardship)
- - ISO certification of commercial, industrial businesses towards efficient resource use (managementt)
- - creation of an integrated transfer station with recycling and compost facility

- - installation of segregation bins within places of high human activity (residential communities, schools, parks/plazas, commercial areas)
- - installation of material recovery facilities (MRF)
- - conduct regular street cleaning (recovery of street garbage)
- - construction of a compost facility or a backyard compost bin

Towardsa Balanced Multi-Core Neighborhood Connected City

Development Strategy

TheConcept

Core Development using the Neighborhood clustering from barangay units, the development strategy focused on the connectivity of prime area of activities.

For the purpose of setting the unit of development, the City focuses on a community or neighborhood scale. The principle here is having neighborhoods as the basic building blocks of communities. A community is a section of a city, primarily a residential area. It usually represents the service area of a high school, and contains a large business center. It can be thought of as a community of neighborhoods because it is usually composed of three to five neighborhoods. In the local context, a neighborhood is about the size ofa barangay, defined by the Local Government Code as having a population of 2,000 persons (rural) or 5,000 (urban).

The Main Theme: Multi-Centered/Multi-Core

The conversion of the urban structure from a single-core concentration to a dispersal of multi-core one rich in local distinctiveness and well-balanced working and living facilities with the design objectives of enhancement of site uniqueness, imageability, proper orientation of

An ideal barangay should have a center and an edge within a 15-minute comfortable walk

activities and circulation, identity, and visual unity.

from the center. Grouped together to form a community, it must be a self-contained, dense,

tightly woven community, that mixes stores, housing, entertainment, civic and recreational uses within walking distance with job creation as an important factor. It will be an alternative to urban sprawl, and should promote orderly urban development. The aim is to put more origin and destination points within an easy walk of a transit stop for inter-city travel while reducing

The priority of development will be given to the development of the progressive international and information oriented city functions. While the promotion in the attractiveness and vitality of the whole city shall always be given importance.

- 1) Business site/ activity to be spread and systematically improved by redevelopment of existing identified known business activity with the "cores" as the center. While the areas along the Roxas Boulevard (waterfront area) is progressing, urban areas with compound functions, including business functions, will be established while paying attention to establishment of enough space for housing.
- 2) Comfortable Residential areas through improved sidewalks and accessibility to public transport. Socio-economic facilities should further be improved through mixed use development and transport-oriented development (TOD) so that distance between place of work and residence will be shortened and medium- and high-rise residential buildings will be built in a better environment with ample open space.

For existing residential areas located between the downtown (old CBD) and the identified subcenters, quality residential structures will be preserved and new residential areas should be provided or replace through redevelopment.

- 3) Attractive Commercial Areas at the Binondo-Escolta-Sta Cruz as well as along Ermita and Malate should be improved as attractive , walkable wide commercial centers enhancing its historic characteristics making the most of its functions for business and culture. These areas are identified as cores and should be developed extensively.

The Urban Core

Due to planned infrastructure development of national government and for the Old CBD (Binondo-Escolta-Sta Cruz) to be properly redeveloped, the Old Bilibid Prison will now be the New Urban Coreofthe City. All major mass transport ofthe city will pass through this area

In order to discourage an excessive concentration of business functions at Old CBD, the Floor Area Ratio (FAR) will be controlled and other areas to follow new restrictions.

7

i

The Subcenters - subcenters are identified based on distinct capability to generate business activity and serves asa traffic node that can create a mass activity within a 600 meter radial distance. It is expected that identified sub-centers will trigger independence based on the livelihood it will generate and interdependence based on proper linkages.

Buhayin ang Maynila! &

- a) Industrial Mixed Use Subcenter located at the northwestern area of reclamation area where the Manila Harbour Centre is situated alongside with the Smokey Mountain Development. This location also serves as a Gateway of industrial activity from the northern side of the city.
- b) Residential Mixed Use Sub center located at crossing of Tayuman and Juan Luna. Small scale industry such as furniture coming from port proliferates in this area surrounded by other neighborhood scale businesses that supplements a wide range of residential development. The Transport Oriented Developmentor TOD is best applied in this area.
- c) University District 1 first batch of university and school clustering located at Espafia and A.H. Lacson. This may be called also as institutional mixed use. All activity patterns here revolve around the schools within its sphere especially in support services needed by students.

University or ‘silicon alley" concept may be introduced here or should now be formalized since it is already proliferating. These are the computer services and reproduction of documents. This cluster generates a lot of information exchange and research and development that should be nurtured by the city and developed as its data bank in sciences and information technology.

- d) Residential Parkway (Transition Center) - located at Ramon Magsaysay Boulevard and Nagtahan area. This areais a clusteringof different land uses from residential to institutional to commercial but will primarily focus on commercial and institutional development at

Other smaller scale commercial areas on each district should be fostered as bright and busy places for local residents’ interchange through enhancement of its construction and creation of open parks for maximum view and preservation of public spaces. Proper

landscaping and streetscape will provide ample security also.

All identified core areas are connected by nested loops to define a wider CBD and its sub-

areas. Visually link sub-areas and the CBD to other areas of activity. To aid orientation, provision of access to public and natural amenities and separation of modes of

LandUse

Commercial

The Proposed Land Use Plan was made with the general purpose of guiding and accomplishing a coordinated, adjusted, and harmonious development of the city. And its environs which will in accordance with present and future needs, best promote health,

Create a vibrant, mixed-use economy by directing downtown office economy into currently under utilized areas or zone-segregated areas, enhancing the city's core, encouraging neighborhood-oriented businesses, strengthening the area's existing

MANILA

COMPREHENSIVE

LAND, USE) PLAN AND ‘YONING — ORDINANCE

safety, morals, orders, convenience, prosperity, and general welfare, as well as efficiency

entertainment and visitor sectors, and ensuring that neighborhood residents share in the

and economy in the process of development, including among other things, adequate provision for traffic, the promotion of safety from fire, flood waters, and other dangers, adequate provision for light and air, the promotion of healthful and convenient distribution of population, the promotion of good civic design and arrangement, wise and efficient expenditures of public funds, and the adequate provision of public utilities and other requirements.

economic benefits of City's revitalization/development.

2005-2020

Commercial mixed use land uses are encouraged along the LRT and PNR lines and the

major roads. Alongside the retention and maintenance of existing commercial facilities, the plan proposes the commercial development along the University district. With the

future development of LRT4 and near completion of LRT 2, City Core commercial

###### Buhayin ang Maynila! )

development will be pushed to the Old Bilibid area promoting a development of Intermodal transport and commercial complex

Residential

Encourage a clustered development style for multi-family and commercial development which is easily and economically supported by governmental facilities and services.

The work area where it is presently concentrated on the western side of the city is devoted to the city's major employment. This heavy concentration of work areas will now be dispersed with the development strategy being proposed to disperse point sources of pollution. The dispersal of commercial activity in the city will bring it into convenient proximity to living areas where energy-efficient interconnecting transit and thoroughfare routes can be designed to insure access back and forth. Convenient proximity to other work areas and where uses accessory to one another will have access to interconnecting routes and should provide sites adequate in size, economic to develop, and attractively situated for the particular uses intended.

The inevitable inmigration from adjacent provinces, a number of development styles may beconsidered;

- a. Adoptland use policies and regulations that provide incentives for the clustering of development and requirements for the dedication of open

space.

- b. Adopt land use policies and regulations that encourage clustered development in locations that are highly serviceable with public facilities to include transportation, sewer and water services, solid waste disposal, fire and police protection, and location which are in close proximity to commercial support facilities and places of employment.

Institutional

The projected population will require more basic service facilities. The plan proposes for the establishment of a wider educational zone called the University District Overlay Zone. The development will comprise convention centers, additional health, education

River are no longer acceptable in the city. The presence of the Oil Depot at Pandacan area will eventually be removed on a by-staged/phased development as agreed between the city and the ‘Big 3' . under the City Ordinance No. 8027, rezoning Pandacan Terminal area to Commercial | from Industrial Il. Based on current study, the removal of the Terminal will take 10 -15 years but; any national issues pertaining to the removal of the Oil Depot shall be resolved between the National and City government. At present an approximately 2.0 hectare linear park development is developed along the Oil Depot area as a buffer zone between the depot and the residential development

For environmental protection and conservation purposes, buffer zones shall be

established between conflicting land uses (i.e., industrial and residential uses). Protected areas include potential and existing tourist spots, rivers and creeks. These

areas must be declared as protected areas and to disallow any form of developmentthat

MANILA: COMPREHENSIVE

will destroy these assets.

AND YONING ORDINANCE

Mixed Use

2005-2020

x

adjacenttoit.

x

The plan proposesa variety of mixed use development along major roads specifically along major transport corridor where the LRT projects are presently and planning to be located. These are along Rizal Avenue up to Taft Avenue, the Espafia Boulevard and Ramon Magsaysay Boulevard. A high density mixed use development is encouraged along these corridors to lessen the transportation trips of residents and commuters from one place to another and at the same time havea diversified tax base for the different activities within a structure or within 600 meters radial distance from point of origin in this case the transport terminals.

Water Bodies / Bay, River, Canals & Esteros

###### Buhayin ang Maynila! @

Along with the on-going Pasig River Rehabilitation Plan, approximately 500 meters on

both sides of the riverbank from its highest water level are zoned as multi-use mixed use

with its ten meter easement planned as linear parks.

Open Space/ Parks / Buffer Zones

This mixed use development is actually encouraged for the whole city depending on the prevailing and use pattern. In the case of the university belt, the plan proposed a wider scope of development into a university district overlay zone. This encourage an institutional mixed use development focusing mainly on the university activities and to encourage surrounding development within the zone to provide commercial and recreational activities and facilities that would complement the students needs.

In enhancing the quality of the "built" environment, the proposed plan will consist of additional parks and playground as needed based on the requirement of the area and population. The absences of space for such development are relegated to development of roof gardens within the city especially on C2 and C3 area.

The Plan proposes also the re-establishment of the Old Burnham plan of having the City as a walkable area with lush greeneries every 600 meters. This is achieved by development of wide sidewalks with appropriate street trees and the revival of the lost parks and plazas which is now presently being undertaken by the City under the Buhayin Ang Maynila Project.

The mixed use development will further strengthen the present 24-hour city activity that will constitute safe and affordable places to work, play, and reside. That is mixing the

existing ingredients (present developments) with sufficient amount of activities (the

Proposed Land Use

massing) and meshing all these developments (sufficiently near each other) to enable the intended changes to occur. Pushing for this pattern of development of mixed use, pedestrian orientedness, retail activities a demand for offices and with addition of jobs, there will be a market for residential development.

Although, as of today, there is a limited open space in the city, these areas are to be enhanced by planting of trees all over these open lands. The existing parks and plazas in the different districts shall be developed as breathing spaces. Construction of more parks will be developed and integrated in the different nodes of the area wherein these open spaces will become greenhearts whereby trees and grass will be planted and maintained. Not only do they enhance the land

MCLUPZO Five (5) Item Plan Hi-Lights

For further implementation guidelines on the Network of Open Spaces, please refer to the Implementing Rules and Regulation of the 2005-2020 Zoning Ordinance.

The following are the Five (5) Item Plan Hi-Lights of the 2005-2020 Manila Comprehensive Land Use Plan and Zoning Ordinance:

Urban Waterfront Development

MANICAS COMPREHENSIVE

I. “ City Beautiful Movement”

Another part of this City Beautiful Movement is creating a Waterfront Development. This

LAND, USE” PUAN AND- YOWING > ORDINANCE 2005-2020

is focused on two main themes:

City Imageability

aesy

- 1. Reinforcing the River's Significance In order to integrate the Pasig river and other major waterways to the community, the following guidelines are encouraged to be implemented:

- - organize land development to visually define the river's linkage to the community
- - focus and delineate the roads and pedestrian ways to emphasize the river and other major waterways
- - create activity centers and centers of interest that celebrate or gives meaning and focus on the waterfront

- 2. Reinforcing the Identity of the WaterfrontArea In order to create design solutions that contribute to the character of the waterfront and acknowledge its heritage, the following guidelines are encouragedto be implemented:

P |

{

This promotes the visual “imageability” of the City according to the Burnham Plan of 1905. As per plan recommendation from Daniel Burnham, it gives emphasis on the

creation and enhancement of wide boulevards, public buildings, landscaped parks and pleasant vistas. It also encourages the connectivity of spaces and places through

###### Buhayin ang Maynila! &

various systems/networks (transport/parkways). But most of all, it is the establishment of a symbolic focus that would identify the City as Manila as well as become its unifying

element. These are the main themes for Place Making revolving around creating a

“sense of place” and distinction within the City

Open Space Network

A part of this connectivity plan is the proposed establishment of the Open Space

Network as reflected in the following map. This Network of Open Space is created based on the Burnham Plan of having an average distance of one (1) kilometer between parks, plazas, and recreational places. This involves a network of sidewalks, paseos, and offroad trails/pathways throughout the city and linking activity centers, commercial uses,

- - incorporate remnants of industrial infrastructure (for old industrial areas that are now converted to other uses)
- - provide facilities for informal gathering places
- - create graceful transitions between the built environment and open spaces

recreation and open space areas, and residential uses. Though the areas identified on

###### Five (5) Plan Hi-Light

the map are part of an initial study, these proposed locations need to have a more indepth review and planning as well. It needs to have more input regarding the walkability and distribution of these proposed opens spaces to the existing ones.

SMART Development Principles

It is one of the objectives of the CLUP to create a SMART plan, a Sustainable, Measurable, Attainable, Realistic, and Time-bounded plan, which defines the core of all the recommendations reflected within the plan. These are all fashioned towards the SMART Development Principles:

These recommended areas for placement of usable open spaces is not necessarily restricted to open spaces on ground level. The implementation may be achieved

The Mixed Use Conceptis described by the Urban Land Institute (ULI) as:

- - structures that have three or more significant revenue-producing uses that Zone Regulations in well-planned projects are mutually supporting;

Ill. Urban Renewal Mobilization

- IV. Safescape Implementation

In creating a safe community, “a safescape”, it involves the following aspects to be integrated within the urban form:

“Eyes on the Street” concept

- - Separating and Joining the Private and the Public Realm
- - Creating a “sense of community”
- - _ Livability and Usability
- - Streetscaping Zoning the Sidewalk
- - Information and orientation
- - Gender sensitivity/gender responsive development
- - Zoning
- - Implementing Rules and Regulation of the Zoning Ordinance/Design and Development Guidelines
- - monitoring and stewardship
- - IEC and capability trainings
- - prevention and mitigation plans
- - disaster proof living zones

- V. Management and Maintenance of City Growth

“Urban Renewal: Towards an Urban Renaissance”

Urban renewal involves the combination of the following criteria in planning an area to result to a more practical and aesthetically pleasing location.

In the physical planning of the City, the Zoning Ordinance is the implementing body of the CLUP.And the basic management measures are those set by the Zoning Ordinance and specifically as set by the FAR provision. This basically prescribes the allowable overall development within the City in terms of density or space coverage, massing, form, and height restrictions of structures.

Development Concepts:

- 1. Business Improvement Districts (BID)
- 2. Mixed Use Development
- 3. Multi-Use Development
- 4. Transport Oriented Development (TOD)
- 5. Great Streets “Sense of Place”; Creating Streets of Character; Urban Design
- 6. New Urbanism adopting the Traditional Neighborhood Design (TND) into the modern urban environment
- 7. Pedestrianization the pedestrian space and community scale (providing human scale and interest to buildings along sidewalks and walkways)
- 8. Histo-Cultural Conservation
- 9. Roof Gardens
- 10. Environmental Design

###### Five (5) Plan Hi-Light

FLOORAREARATIO

Floor Area Ratio (FAR), as per definition, is the ratio between gross floor area of a building and the area of the lot on which it stands, determined by dividing the gross floor area of the building by the area of the lot. The gross floor area of any building should not exceed the prescribed floor area ratio (FAR) multiplied by the lot area. The FAR of the zone is based on its capacity to support development in terms of the absolute level of density that the transportation and other utility networks can support.

Transit-Oriented Developments (TOD)

Transferof Development Rights (TDR) for Citywide Applicability

In all commercial zones (C-2/MXD, C-3/MXD), a building or a structure that is within four hundred (400) meters walking distance from an existing LRT or commuter rail station will be allowed to build three (3) FAR higher than their maximum FAR specified in this Zoning Ordinance, PROVIDED, That the building owner/s or developer/s should build and maintain, at his cost, a direct, elevated as to being in the same level as the terminal, well-ventilated, sheltered pedestrian link from his building to the immediate structure of the transit station/terminal building as approved by the Building Official and in consultation with
